# 3D building data for Asia and the Middle East (East Asia, China, Mongolia, South-East Asia, Central Asia, South Asia, the Middle East, Cyprus)

Run date 2026-11-13. Read-only research (task #1327) for Scenic Roads. Method: four sub-agents (Sonnet, medium) by area (A: Japan, Koreas, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau; B: China, Mongolia and the global sets; C: South-East and Central Asia; D: South Asia and the Middle East incl. Cyprus), run in two rounds; the second round re-checked every provincial, state and national portal with addresses read from fetched pages (lists, Wikidata official-website fields, search results), never guessed. Each fetched pages and API answers as `scenic-roads/0.1 (personal offline map)`, sampled Overture release 2026-10-21.0 (512 building parts, 2,533,842,612 rows) and opened real files where it could. I merged their parts into the region tables (the Europe-E report's 12 columns; each part's text is its agent's own, headings renumbered) and checked every table row for its header's cell count. Russia, Turkey and the Caucasus are in buildings-europe-e.md.

## 1. Headline

- **Asia has almost no open, per-building height or storey source; the few real ones are listed here.** Overture heights across about 80 sampled boxes are OpenStreetMap tags only (no government or modelled supplier appears), mostly under 5 % of buildings: Tokyo 1.9 %, Seoul 4.0 %, Hong Kong 3.2 %, every mainland China box 0.0-1.6 % (Nanjing 6.6 %), Ulaanbaatar 0.1 %, 35 of 42 South-East and Central Asian boxes under 1 %, 12 of 14 South Asian and Middle Eastern boxes under 2 %. Exceptions: Osaka 59.2 % (its OSM heights equal PLATEAU's, median difference 0.0 m, 97 % within 20 %, so OSM Osaka looks like a PLATEAU import), Singapore centre 12.4 %, Dubai Downtown 9.9 %, Taipei 9.6 %. Storeys are better: Hong Kong 51 %, Taipei 40 %, Astana 39.1 %, Singapore 29.7 %, Dhaka 23.9 %, Tel Aviv 16.9 %, but under 10 % in most of China, India, Pakistan, Iran, Saudi Arabia.
- **Japan, PLATEAU is the best open set in Asia.** 306 city or ward units in 45 prefectures (none in Yamagata or Fukui; LOD1 in all 306, LOD2 in 247, LOD3 in 18, newest year per unit 2020-2025), PLATEAU Site Policy (free reuse including commercial) on 487 of 495 G-spatial packages; Tokyo Metropolis republishes the 2025 3D map for all 23 wards and 26 Tama municipalities, CC BY. Opened: Tokyo 2020 tiles add a height to 98.3 % of source buildings (92.2 % match an Overture footprint; 49 pairs, median absolute difference 5.6 m); Chuo-ku 2025 adds a height to 97.5 % and storeys to 1,864; Osaka Umeda 2025 adds a height to 47.8 % (404 pairs, median difference 0.0 m). Tiles can be pulled from the 1.5-5 GB zips by HTTP range. No prefecture has a better building product (Oita's and Shizuoka's survey packages are zone layers, no buildings). GSI's 基盤地図情報 outlines need a login (not used); its vector tile building layer has no height or storeys (decoded), PDL 1.0 with a source credit.
- **Hong Kong's Lands Department Building layer is usable for heights once parts are combined.** Anonymous WFS, 342,233 polygons, one per building part (in the 15,020-feature Central/Wan Chai box: 11,814 Tower, 2,327 Podium, 628 Open-sided, 251 Temporary), BaseHeight and TopHeight in metres above Principal Datum (inferred from the values); a tower on a podium has its base at the podium roof. Matching Overture's whole buildings to the nearest polygon gave the 23 m median (39.4 m to any type, 57 m to podiums) because the match picks a podium; Top minus the lowest touching Base on Tower polygons gives a median 2.6 m difference (signed +0.3 m, 85.9 % within 20 %, 305 pairs). HK's top includes masts (Central Plaza 380.7 against 309 m in OSM). Tower storeys agree with OSM's in 47.0 % of 2,799 pairs (73.5 % within one). DATA.GOV.HK terms: attribution, commercial use allowed.
- **South Korea:** the national polygon sets (GIS건물통합정보 and 연속수치지형도_건물 KOGL type 1; 건물통합정보_마스터 type 4, non-commercial and no changes; the daily, general and building-age sets "no limit") are known from their data.go.kr records only: V-World and nsdi.go.kr did not answer and no login was used. Opened instead: Seoul's 건물 태양에너지 등급도 2018 (anonymous, 161 MB, 1,029,872 building points, 층수 on 65.5 %, 건물높이 exactly 3.5 m times storeys so derived; KOGL 1 with an unnamed third-party rights note) adds storeys to 13,585 of 14,639 matched Overture buildings (equal in 55.5 % of 1,050 pairs); Gwacheon's 새주소건물 (5,029 polygons with above and below-ground floors, no height) adds floors to 2,017 of 2,315 matched, equal in 98.5 % of 268 pairs. None of the 17 metropolitan or provincial units has a polygon layer with heights. **Taiwan:** NLSC's LOD1-LOD3 model is sold; Taichung's 114 building shapefile (1,263,194 unit-cut polygons, floors in field `B1D`, Taiwan Open Government Data Licence v1, anonymous Drive link) adds floors to 14,705 matched buildings (equal in 72.6 % of 5,058 pairs, 93.7 % within one); Taipei's permit overlay (10,051 polygons) adds storeys to 1,740; New Taipei, Kaohsiung, Tainan and Taoyuan have no footprint or floors set on data.gov.tw. **Macau:** data.gov.mo's whole catalogue (1,377 datasets, read through its API) has no building geometry. North Korea: nothing.
- **China and Mongolia: no open government 3D, footprint-with-height or storey source was found.** Real-scene 3D China is served over government networks with no public download; Tianditu answers 418 (bot block, not circumvented); Baidu, Amap and Tencent terms bar caching; Google 3D Tiles bar offline use. Beijing's catalogue (4,730 datasets, all paged through its search call) has no footprint or 3D layer; Guangdong's portal (carrying Guangzhou, Shenzhen, Zhuhai, Foshan) lists Shenzhen's 建筑物数据 as open without conditions: 310,696 rows of building code, name and address, no geometry, height or storeys (10 rows opened); Zhejiang (24,322 entries) has Wenzhou-Ruian's per-building ground height, roof height and floors, 491 records without coordinates; Yunnan says "coming soon"; Shanghai (412) and Tianjin (403) are blocked and about 25 other provincial and city hosts resolve but time out (named in sections 8 and 12). Mongolia's opendata.gov.mn (738 files, 735 CC0 and 3 CC BY, read through its API) has no footprints, heights or storeys. The only per-building heights are modelled national sets: CMAB (31 M buildings, CC BY 4.0) and 3D-GloBFP (2020, CC BY 4.0). Opened: 3D-GloBFP Shanghai adds a height to 99.5 % of 22,297 buildings (63.9 % match an Overture footprint); CMAB Shanghai 99.6 % (9,126; 71.6 %), CMAB Beijing 99.5 % (10,060; 60.1 %); the two sets disagree in central Shanghai (6,880 pairs, median absolute difference 10.8 m, correlation 0.38) and sit 19-49 m under OSM-tagged towers there: a typical-height fallback, not truth. GlobalBuildingAtlas covers China (101 of 104 5-degree tiles; the rest open sea) and Mongolia (all 21); CC BY-NC 4.0 except its ODbL polygon part; its Ulaanbaatar tile opened: 18,898 buildings in the Overture box all with a height (median 3.4 m), against Overture's heights on 28.
- **South-East Asia:** Indonesia's BIG serves six I3S LoD1 building layers anonymously (Makassar 580,974 feature records, South Sulawesi 580,771, Manado 190,094, Kendari 156,186, Bantaeng 91,309, Bitung 62,051; Makassar median 7.5 m, max 136 m; Bantaeng's heights are clipped to 1-10 m); 18 of 25 service folders open, 7 need a token (not approached); no licence text found, so not cleared for caching. Opened earlier: Makassar adds a height to 38,588 of 38,604 matched buildings. Jakarta's ArcGIS server (found in the Jakarta Satu 3D page's script) answers anonymously: Master_Bangunan 1,741,931 building polygons, floors on only 11,362 (0.65 %), no metre height; a roof-form set has metre heights on 62,223 parts and a Kota Tua layer on 25,165 buildings; no licence read. Balikpapan's footprints on the East Kalimantan portal (185,364 polygons, no height; "Other (Public Domain)") are the one cleared set. Singapore's data.gov.sg gives HDB footprints (13,509) and property information with maximum floor level (13,392 blocks) under the Singapore Open Data Licence v1.0; NUS's hdb3d CityJSON (10,966 objects) opened: adds a height to 72.8 % of Toa Payoh, storeys agree in 92.6 % of 580 pairs, heights are extrusions from storeys. Sarawak's terms allow commercial use, combining and caching with attribution, but its catalogue has no building layer. Nothing open with heights or storeys was found for Malaysia, Thailand, Vietnam, the Philippines (NAMRIA's WMS has built-up zones only), Myanmar, Cambodia (CC BY-SA footprints), Laos, Brunei, Timor-Leste, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan (APIs searched) or the other Central Asian states (no portal found or host unreachable).
- **South Asia and the Middle East:** Abu Dhabi's ADSDI layer 353 (392,495 polygons, storeys on 50.1 %, anonymous REST) adds storeys to 62.6 % of its central box (46.8 % equal to Overture, 79.2 % within a floor); the ADSDI service has no licence text, and the Abu Dhabi Government Open Data Licence v1.0 (attribution only, commercial use allowed) applies if the layer is on that platform, not confirmed. Beer Sheva's data.gov.il buildings (26,350 with floors; "Other (Open)", the city portal says "Other (Public Domain)") add storeys to 89.7 % (68.0 % equal). Cyprus's DLS INSPIRE buildings as a Zenodo GeoPackage (563,446 with number of floors, CC BY 4.0; 61 % of values are 1) add storeys to 90.5 % of the Nicosia box. None carries a metric height. India has no open building set found: GOBS is request-only, non-commercial and by approved purpose (its terms page read); data.gov.in is GODL-India but its catalogue is unreadable; Pune's 238 datasets have no building layer; the state and city portals read are in sections 8 and 12. Dubai's digital twin is not public and Dubai Pulse's floor-level table (332 MB, no coordinates) is by permission request; Bahrain's SLRB LOD1-1.5 building data (478 blocks, ±20 cm) is sold; Qatar (2,082 datasets, CC BY) and Ajman (195, CC BY 4.0) hold statistics only. 
- **Global sets (section 6):** Google Open Buildings and its 2.5D Temporal set cover South and South-East Asia (not China, Mongolia or the Middle East); Microsoft covers Mongolia and the rest of Asia except China (CDLA Permissive 2.0); GlobalBuildingAtlas heights and LoD1 are CC BY-NC 4.0 (out); GHSL and WSF3D are coarse rasters.
- **ODbL mixing:** CC BY 4.0, KOGL type 1, the Singapore Open Data Licence, DATA.GOV.HK terms, PLATEAU Site Policy, the Taiwan open-data licence v1, GODL (Abu Dhabi) and CC0 are attribution-only and allow a personal offline cache; the OSM wiki rates CC BY "likely incompatible" for contributing into OSM itself (a community page), so a separate attributed layer is the cleaner reading. Not usable: Baidu, Amap, Tencent, Google 3D Tiles, Shenzhen's platform (no redistribution, withdrawable), Taiwan's priced model, Korea's non-commercial master, GBA heights, Dubai's twin, GOBS (non-commercial), Bahrain's data.
- **Measured from opened files (agents' figures):** PLATEAU (Tokyo 2020, Chuo-ku 2025, Osaka 2025), 15,020 Hong Kong building parts, Taipei overlay, Taichung's shapefile, Seoul's solar table, Gwacheon, CMAB (Shanghai, Beijing), 3D-GloBFP (Shanghai), GBA's Ulaanbaatar tile, SinoBF-1 (Xiantao), Singapore (HDB tables, NUS hdb3d, URA layer), BIG's layers, Jakarta's services, Balikpapan's shapefile, Abu Dhabi (4,546 features), Beer Sheva, Cyprus, Beijing's and Mongolia's whole catalogues, Macau's catalogue. Everything else is described from pages or records only, including the Korean national sets, GOBS, NUIS and Dubai's twin.
- **Still unverified (section 12):** the blocked or unreachable Chinese portals (Shanghai, Tianjin and about 25 hosts that time out), data.gov.in's catalogue and the Indian state layer lists, V-World and nsdi.go.kr, Saudi Arabia, Kuwait, Jordan, Iraq, Syria, Yemen and Palestine, Pakistan's provinces other than Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, Bangladesh, Bhutan, Maldives, Afghanistan, Nepal, Tel Aviv, Sharjah and Umm Al Quwain, Malaysia's state portals, Thailand's and Vietnam's live portals, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan, Laos, Timor-Leste, BIG's and Jakarta's licence terms. Many hosts refuse connections from here, so a gap may be the network, not absence of data.

### 2.2 South Korea

The national building layers (polygons joined to register attributes) are downloaded from V-World, which did not answer from this Mac (HTTP 502, then no connection), so their field lists are unread; their licence types come from each record's JSON-LD on data.go.kr (read). The anonymous files found are on data.go.kr itself (the page's own download button works without a login: `selectFileDataDownload.do` answers status true, `check-limit.json` answers needCaptcha false, `fileDownload.do` returns the zip) and on data.seoul.go.kr (a form POST to datafile.seoul.go.kr). Two were opened and compared with Overture: Seoul's 2018 building solar-grade file and Gwacheon's new-address building shapefile. No account was created.

BaseHeight is the foot of that part: for a tower on a podium it is the podium's roof level (2,474 of the 3,095 towers lying over a podium start within 1.5 m of that podium's TopHeight), so TopHeight minus BaseHeight is the part's own height and a tower standing on a podium reads far lower than the whole building; the whole building's height above ground is TopHeight minus the lowest BaseHeight among the parts touching it. Storeys is the part's own count (8,701 of 11,814 towers have one) | 342,233 features territory-wide; the box returned 15,020, all `Status` Active; 14,298 have a positive TopHeight minus BaseHeight; Storeys on 10,404 | sample rows show DateCreate 30/4/2005 and DateStamp 12/7/2013 and 6/10/2020; ### 3.2 China: provincial and municipal open-data platforms read

Method. The portal addresses come from a fetched list of local platforms (the 2021 compilation at https://www.cnblogs.com/Deyunshe/p/14850638.html, which names about 60 provincial, city and ministry platforms); data.gov.cn itself does not connect from here and Wayback was offline during the run. Of the hosts on that list that were tried, six answered and had a catalogue I could query: Beijing, Guangdong (which carries Guangzhou, Shenzhen, Zhuhai, Foshan and other cities), Zhejiang (Hangzhou, Wenzhou, Shaoxing and others), plus the home pages of Yunnan, Henan and Suzhou (no catalogue behind them). Each answering catalogue's own search call was found in its page scripts and called as a browser would (anonymous, one request a second or slower). The rows below are the units where something building-related exists; the units with nothing, and those whose hosts did not answer, are in NOTHING BETTER.

| Jurisdiction / product | Product, version | Publisher | Holds | Heights or storeys: how measured, what they mean | Completeness | Year | Format and access | Licence; cache?; ODbL-mixable? | Footprint/coverage-index URL | Vs Overture | Evidence URL(s) fetched |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shenzhen / 深圳市规划和自然资源局建筑物数据 (building data), listed on the Guangdong provincial platform 开放广东 and on Shenzhen's own platform | Guangdong catalogue entry resId 3070220300144/001436 (a second entry of the same name, id 6542605354100054, is its API-type item); published 2023-12-28, data updated 2023-11-10 | Shenzhen Municipal Planning and Natural Resources Bureau; registered by the Shenzhen Government Services and Data Administration (深圳市政务服务数据管理局) | 310,696 rows, one per building: building code (`bldg_no`, 19 digits starting 4403), current name (`nowname`), address (`bldaddr`); the entry says the buildings opened since 2016, theme urban construction. OPENED: the first 10 rows through the catalogue's anonymous preview call (all three fields are text; the names are housing blocks, private houses and factories). The 2023 Egger et al. paper names a "3D building points" set from Shenzhen's platform; this entry has no points, so that is a different item and was not found | none: no height, storeys, area or coordinates in the three fields | 310,696 records for the city, no geometry, so it cannot place a building on its own; the entry's file-size field says 316522 (unit not stated) | 2016-2023 | csv, json, rdf, xlsx, xml per the catalogue; the entry says 无条件开放 (open without conditions) at level 普通开放 (ordinary open); the preview is anonymous; the full download was not tried. So a personal cache only while the data stays online, no redistribution: not ODbL-mixable. These are feature records: the Makassar leaf nodes repeat OBJECTIDs (see OVERLAP), so distinct buildings are fewer | Badan Informasi Geospasial (BIG), ArcGIS Server geoservices.big.go.id (folder Hosted) | LoD1 building prisms with fields OBJECTID, FCODE, NAMOBJ, JNSBGN (building class), FGSBGN (building function), NDSMHMIN, NDSMHMAX, DSMHMIN, DSMHMAX, DTMH, REMARK, METADATA | metres: NDSMHMAX appears to be the maximum of the normalized DSM over the footprint; DSMHMAX the maximum surface height; DTMH the terrain height (my reading of the field names; the layer has no description). In 12 Makassar leaf nodes (5,845 features): NDSMHMAX median 6.7 m, maximum 64.0 m. Layer-wide NDSMHMAX from the statistics endpoints (median read off the histogram, approximate): Makassar median 7.5 m, mean 7.9, maximum 136.3; Manado 7.4 / 8.1 / 112.2; Kendari 5.9 / 6.3 / 75.7; Bitung 4.8 / 5.3 / 70.9; Bantaeng minimum 1.00, maximum 10.0, mean 5.3 (a range clipped to 1 to 10 m, so it is a capped or classed value, not a measurement). DSMHMAX and DTMH are elevations above the geoid (Bantaeng DSMHMAX mean 280 m), not heights above ground | full coverage of the city extents: Makassar 119.37..119.59 E, -5.28..-4.99; Manado 124.78..125.01 E, 1.40..1.57; Kendari 122.44..122.63 E, -4.09..-3.90; Bantaeng 119.83..120.10 E; Bitung 125.09..125.22 E | 2024 to 2025 | I3S REST with Draco-compressed geometry and binary attribute buffers; no token needed for the layers read; no feature-query endpoint (queries return the layer description) | no licence or copyright text on any of the six services (copyrightText null; the layer description is only "Bangunan LOD 1"). BIG's portal terms could not be read: Ina-Geoportal (tanahair.indonesia.go.id/portal-web) is a JavaScript app whose bundle calls sign-up and sign-in endpoints and carries a bot-challenge site key (no account made, challenge not approached); the www.big.go.id product and service pages (Ina Geoportal, Layanan Rp.0, Tata Cara Pembelian Produk, Layanan Jasa dan Produk Geospasial) show menus and no licence text in the HTML; the BIG regulations a search named (Peraturan BIG 5/2025, 3/2024) were unreachable (peraturan.go.id refused the connection, peraturan.bpk.go.id answered 403). ### 5.6 Pakistan, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka, Nepal, Bhutan, Maldives, Afghanistan, Iran, Iraq, Syria, Lebanon, Jordan, Saudi Arabia, Qatar, Kuwait, Oman, Yemen

No open official building outlines with heights or storeys were reached for these jurisdictions. Two closed-licence leads are in the table above: Bahrain's SLRB topographic data (footprints and rooftops at LOD1 to LOD1.5, sold under a licence agreement) and Dubai Municipality's per-building floor tables (no geometry, access by request). Every other jurisdiction is named in NOTHING BETTER or UNVERIFIED with the pages read or the routes tried.

| Global / GlobalBuildingAtlas (GBA) | GBA.Polygon 2.75 B buildings, GBA.LoD1 2.68 B with heights, GBA.Height 3 m raster; 2019 PlanetScope | TUM (Zhu lab) | footprints, per-building heights, LoD1 models, height raster | ML predicted heights; height RMSE 5.5 m global and 5.9 m in Asia; only two Asian cities in the height training data and underestimation in some Asian high-rise areas noted | height completeness above 97 % of listed buildings. Coverage of China and Mongolia, read from the record's index files (lod1.geojson, height_zip.geojson, height_tif.geojson, fetched by rsync from the mediaTUM record's public representative share): 101 of the 104 5-degree LoD1 and height tiles in the 70-135 E, 15-55 N box exist (the three absent ones, at 125 E 15 N, 130 E 15 N and 130 E 20 N, are open sea), and all 21 tiles in Mongolia's 85-120 E, 40-55 N box; the 0.2-degree height tiles number 30,401 in China's bounding box and 3,210 in Mongolia's. The Source Cooperative Parquet copy sizes the same tiles: 60.4 GB for the 101 China-box tiles and 0.77 GB for the 21 Mongolia-box tiles (the Ulaanbaatar tile e105_n50_e110_n45 is 52 MB with 466,196 buildings) | 2019 (PlanetScope); data generation ended 2025-04-30; record published 2025-09-02 | mediaTUM record (doi 10.14459/2025mp1782307): the full set is 36 TB in 1,844 files over rsync or FTP with public credentials printed on the record page, and an 8 GB representative share of index files; GBA.ODbLPolygon and GBA.Polygon/LoD1 on HuggingFace (not used here); a Parquet copy of GBA.LoD1 on Source Cooperative (922 tiles, 224.7 GB, anonymous https, per-tile files; OPENED: the Ulaanbaatar tile, columns source, id, height, var, region, bbox, geometry) | split: GBA.ODbLPolygon is ODbL; GBA.Polygon, GBA.LoD1 and GBA.Height are CC BY-NC 4.0 (non-commercial; the README warns combining them may have licence implications); code is MIT with Commons Clause. A personal offline map may use NC data only if truly non-commercial; the ODbL part is mixable but holds only OSM and Microsoft footprints. Per component, as read: the mediaTUM record says licence by-nc (CC BY-NC 4.0) and that downloading means agreeing to the viewer's terms of use; those terms (https://tubvsig-so2sat-vm1.srv.mwn.de/terms_of_use.html) say the dataset is CC BY-NC 4.0 (attribution, non-commercial) and that building polygons from OSM and Microsoft are ODbL (attribution, share-alike), plus a clause against unlawful, discriminatory or privacy-infringing use; the README (rewritten 2026-02-01) splits the data into GBA.ODbLPolygon (ODbL), GBA.Polygon and GBA.LoD1 (CC BY-NC 4.0) and GBA.Height (CC BY-NC 4.0). The Source Cooperative Parquet copy states no licence on its page or README, mixes the sources in each row (the `source` column holds osm, ms, 3dglobfp and ours2, so ODbL rows can be split from BY-NC rows), and credits mediaTUM. For a personal offline map (non-commercial, cached on one's own machines) the BY-NC parts are usable with attribution; redistribution to others, or any commercial use, would not be | https://mediatum.ub.tum.de/1782307 (record), lod1.geojson, height_zip.geojson and height_tif.geojson on the record's representative share (rsync://dataserv.ub.tum.de/m1782307.rep/), and https://data.source.coop/tge-labs/globalbuildingatlas-lod1/ (S3-style listing, one parquet per 5-degree tile) | OPENED the Ulaanbaatar tile (e105_n50_e110_n45, 466,196 buildings; sources osm 373,725, 3dglobfp 55,570, ms 21,504, ours2 15,397; all have a height, 465,245 above 0). In the Ulaanbaatar box (106.89..106.94 x 47.89..47.94, centres by bbox) it has 18,898 buildings against Overture's 19,623, every one with a height: median 3.4 m, 10th/90th percentile 1.5/14.8 m (14,725 from osm footprints, 4,173 from 3dglobfp footprints); Overture has a height on 28 and storeys on 1,503 there. The heights are modelled (RMSE 5.5 m global, 5.9 m in Asia per the paper) and the low median shows the box is mostly small buildings (no storey data to check it against). It is the only per-building height found covering Ulaanbaatar; for China it is a third modelled source beside 3D-GloBFP and CMAB, and no China tile was opened. Footprint-to-footprint matching to Overture was not run | https://github.com/zhu-xlab/GlobalBuildingAtlas ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/zhu-xlab/GlobalBuildingAtlas/main/README.md ; https://mediatum.ub.tum.de/1782307 ; https://tubvsig-so2sat-vm1.srv.mwn.de/terms_of_use.html ; https://source.coop/tge-labs/globalbuildingatlas-lod1 ; https://data.source.coop/tge-labs/globalbuildingatlas-lod1/e105_n50_e110_n45.parquet ; https://essd.copernicus.org/articles/17/6647/2025/ |

## 7. Notes per jurisdiction

### 7.1 East Asia (Japan, Koreas, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau)

**Japan**

PLATEAU (MLIT). The catalogue API (7,776 datasets of 24 types; 1,065 of them buildings, all as 3D Tiles, each with its CityGML zip on G-spatial) lists 306 city or ward units in 45 prefectures; Yamagata and Fukui have none. 大阪府 (14): 和泉市 2023 L1; 堺市 2024 L2; 大阪市 2025 L3; 守口市 2025 L1; 岸和田市 2024 L1; 忠岡町 2020 L2; 摂津市 2020 L2; 東大阪市 2024 L2; 柏原市 2022 L2; 池田市 2025 L2; 河内長野市 2025 L2; 豊中市 2020 L2; 門真市 2025 L1; 高槻市 2020 L2

What the numbers mean. In the 2020-era Tokyo v2 tiles, `measuredHeight` is the only height and there are no storeys. In 2023 and later files `storeysAboveGround` is filled; "9999" (storeys) and "-9999" (height) mean unknown (Osaka Umeda tile: 312 of 981 buildings have 9999 storeys, 108 have -9999 height; Chuo-ku tile: 117 and 52). Heights are PLATEAU's `measuredHeight`, presumably from the roof highest point over ground; I did not verify the definition (spec document not read). Not opened: LOD3/LOD4 files, 3D Tiles, MVT. Not read: the PLATEAU product-spec PDF (so no statement of how the height is measured).

Heights already inside Overture. In the Osaka Umeda tile PLATEAU and Overture's OSM heights are equal (404 pairs, median diff 0.0 m, 97.0 % within 20 %), so OSM Osaka has taken its heights from PLATEAU; Overture carries 59 % heights in the Osaka box and 1.9 % in the Tokyo box. Where Overture lacks them (Tokyo), PLATEAU adds a height to 97-98 % of the source buildings in the tiles opened.

GSI. 基盤地図情報 (building outlines, 建築物の外周線) needs a user registration and login on its download service (read on https://www.gsi.go.jp/kiban/, updated 2026-03-07), so it was not downloaded and no account was created; nothing was circumvented. Its terms: the GSI content terms (revised 2025-11-20) put the site's content under PDL 1.0 with a source credit, and the same page notes that 基本測量成果 (which 基盤地図情報 is) may need a Survey Act application for copying or use. The vector tiles are anonymous: the building layer of a decoded z15 tile has 471 polygons carrying only `ftCode` 3111, `lvOrder` and `orgGILvl`, so GSI's open building geometry has no height or storeys; the optimal_bvmap README gives the credit "国土地理院最適化ベクトルタイル" and calls the release experimental, not 基本測量成果.

Prefectures. Each of the 47 prefectures was searched in BODIK (the local-government CKAN at data.bodik.jp) with two queries; per prefecture I kept only datasets whose publisher name matches the prefecture. Hits: Oita (11 cities' 都市計画基礎調査 packages, opened one: zone aggregates, no building polygons), Osaka (HTML pages for Higashiosaka and Hirakata 都市計画基礎調査), Fukuoka (結果 page), Shizuoka (都市計画基本図 PDFs), Wakayama (PLATEAU page). G-spatial's point clouds exist for Shizuoka, Kanagawa, Yamanashi and Ishikawa (Noto). Details in NOTHING BETTER. A G-spatial query for 都市計画基礎調査 returned 57 packages, including Shizuoka's 32 city and town packages (CC BY): the Mishima package's land-use ZIP was downloaded and holds 21,160 land-use polygons and no building layer, and the packages' notes send building-use surveys to each municipality.

**South Korea**

The national building layers (GIS건물통합정보, 건물통합정보_마스터, 연속수치지형도_건물, 일별 and 일반집합 sets, 건축물연령정보) are V-World downloads; V-World (www.vworld.kr) and the NSDI portal (nsdi.go.kr) did not answer from this Mac (502, then no connection), so whether those shapefiles are anonymous, and their field lists, are unread; each record's licence is read from its data.go.kr JSON-LD: 건물통합정보_마스터 is Type 4 (non-commercial, no changes), GIS건물통합정보 and 연속수치지형도_건물 Type 1 (attribution), the daily and general-building sets "제한 없음". The register itself (건축물대장 title section, with 높이, 지상층수, 지하층수) is an API or table without geometry (data.go.kr 건축HUB 15134735, licence "제한 없음"). What is anonymous and open was opened. Seoul: the city's 건물 태양에너지 등급도 (2018) is a one-row-per-building CSV of 1,029,872 buildings with 층수 and 건물높이 and a centroid point; the height is 3.5 m times the storeys, so only the storey count is information; 34.5 % of rows have neither. Against Overture's Seoul box it adds storeys to 13,585 of 14,639 matched buildings; where both have storeys they are equal in 55.5 % (within one in 80.6 %). Gwacheon's new-address building polygons (5,029, with above-ground and basement floors, no height) add floors to 2,017 of 2,315 matched Overture buildings and agree on floors in 98.5 % of 268 pairs. The metropolitan and provincial portals' addresses come from data.go.kr's own list of regional portals (Seoul data.seoul.go.kr, Gyeonggi data.gg.go.kr, Busan data.busan.go.kr, Daegu data.daegu.go.kr, Daejeon openlab.daejeon.go.kr, Incheon data.incheon.go.kr, Ulsan data.ulsan.go.kr, Gwangju bigdata.gwangju.go.kr, Sejong www.sejong.go.kr, Chungbuk data.chungbuk.go.kr); Seoul's catalogue was searched directly through its own search form (114 hits for 건물; the building-shaped ones listed in TABLES), and the others through data.go.kr's file search (공간정보 건물, 새주소건물, 건축물 공간정보 shp, 건물 층수 SHP) and two web searches, which gave city and county tables (Yongin, Wonju, Dangjin, Goesan, Gwangmyeong, Gwangju-si, Gurye, Gwacheon) and the Busan and Jeonnam sets already in TABLES. Seoul's 서울시 건물 위치정보 ended 2021 (Type 3) and its register tables are APIs without geometry; the S-Map 3D viewer's data was not found as a file or API (see UNVERIFIED). Note that data.go.kr already lists "전남광주통합특별시" (the merged Jeonnam-Gwangju entity) as a publisher name on some records.

**Taiwan**

The NLSC national 3D building model (LOD1 to LOD3) is a priced product, not open data; its price page was read. The usable open sources are municipal: Taichung's building shapefile (1,263,194 unit-cut polygons with `B1D` above-ground floors, documented in the dataset's own code sheet; opened, and its floors agree with Overture's in 72.6 % of 5,058 pairs, within one in 93.7 %, while adding floors to 14,705 matched buildings) and Taipei's building-permit overlay (3.22 MB, 10,051 polygons: a partial set, not a city-wide layer). data.gov.tw's catalogue API works with the body its own search page sends (`bool: [{fulltext: {value: ...}}]`, found in the site's script; a plain keyword field is ignored): searches for 建物, 建築物, 3D, 樓層, 輪廓, 量體, 三維 and per-city forms listed building footprints only for Taichung and Taipei; New Taipei, Kaohsiung, Tainan and Taoyuan have tables (permits, controlled buildings, parking) and utility layers. The Taipei citizen suggestion for a 3D building model is answered as "已回覆" with no dataset; Taichung's 帝國糖廠 三維模型 and Taipei's 宗教建築3D模型庫 are single-site models.

**Hong Kong**

The Lands Department Building layer is the one open, anonymous, per-building-part source with a level-based height and storeys in this area. It holds one polygon per part: Tower, Podium, Open-sided Structure or Temporary Structure, each with a BaseHeight and TopHeight in metres. The datum is not named on the pages read, but the levels fit metres above Principal Datum (Jardine House 3.7 to 182.0; a hillside podium 132.2 to 168.0). For a tower on a podium the BaseHeight is the podium roof (2,474 of 3,095 towers over a podium), so Top minus Base is the tower part's own height; the whole building's height above ground is Top minus the lowest Base among its touching parts. Compared with Overture's whole-building OSM heights, the large median difference (39 m) when each Overture building is matched to the largest-overlap polygon of any type is a part-versus-whole artifact: that polygon is often the podium. Matched to Tower polygons, Top minus the lowest touching Base differs by a median 2.6 m (signed +0.3 m, 85.9 % within 20 % of 305 pairs), and Top minus the tower's own Base differs by 11.7 m (podium lost). Central Plaza's HK top (380.7) is higher than the OSM 309 m because the layer includes the mast. The layer is usable for heights when parts are combined as above, and for storeys per part (towers: equal to OSM's in 47.0 % of 2,799 pairs, within one in 73.5 %).

**Data-quality notes on the Overture sample**

Overture's `sources` entries here carry no per-property pointer (property field empty), so a height comes from the record's `dataset` ("OpenStreetMap"); the ODbL OSM records carry every height in the boxes, and no Japanese, Korean, Taiwanese or Hong Kong government dataset is named as a source in any of the 8 boxes. A third supplier, `doi:10.5281/zenodo.8174931` (the East Asian buildings dataset, CLSM), supplies footprints only (no heights or floors in any box), and in Busan and Kaohsiung it supplies 75 % and 74 % of the footprints.

### 7.2 China, Mongolia and the global sets

**China**

- **Opened.** CMAB (figshare record 27992417, CC BY 4.0, version 7 of 2025-04-20, 37 files, 17.0 GB) Shanghai zip (337 MB, 21 city shapefiles) and Beijing zip (249 MB, 15 shapefiles); attributes `Height`, `Function`, `Age`, `Quality`, `Structure`, `Style`, `merged_id`, EPSG:3857, polygons. The shapefiles have no storey field although the metadata sheet lists "Floor". The metadata sheet says the height field is "Building Height, m" and the building count is 31 million over 3,667 natural (built-up) cities; the unit of the roof (eave or ridge) is not stated on the records read. The 3D-GloBFP tile 2435 (Shanghai, 68 MB zip, 822,221 polygons, EPSG:4326, fields Height, type, id) was also opened.
- **Footprints.** Overture's China footprints in the central boxes come from "doi:10.5281/zenodo.8174931": Shi et al., "A first high-quality vector data of buildings in East Asian countries" (China, Japan, North Korea, South Korea, Mongolia; Zenodo, one 23.1 GB zip, CC BY 4.0, 2023-07-22). It has no heights. 3D-GloBFP and CMAB heights sit on the same family of footprints.
- **Heights are modelled.** 3D-GloBFP (XGBoost, 2020) and CMAB (XGBoost bootstrapped, 31 M buildings) are statistical heights; CNBH-10m is a 10 m raster. In central Shanghai the two disagree: on 6,880 paired buildings CMAB minus 3D-GloBFP is +4.5 m median signed, median absolute 10.8 m, ratio 1.25, within 20 % for 21.4 %, correlation 0.38. So a modelled height is a typical-height guide, not a per-building fact.
- **Government.** The only central statement is the 2022 notice for real-scene 3D China, read through a Wayback capture: it sets targets and says the data will be served over internet, government and classified networks; nothing is published for download. Tianditu is a token-keyed tile service; the terms were not readable (418) so no legal reading is claimed. Shenzhen's platform terms were read in full (free but non-transferable, may be withdrawn).
- **Provincial and city platforms.** Four catalogues could be queried (Beijing, Guangdong, Zhejiang, and Mongolia's national portal), by calling the JSON search each portal's own page scripts use. Beijing: all 4,730 datasets paged and checked, none a footprint, height or 3D layer. Guangdong (also carrying Guangzhou, Shenzhen, Zhuhai and others): Shenzhen's 建筑物数据 (310,696 buildings) opened through the anonymous preview and holds only code, name and address, no geometry or height; the other hits are registers. Zhejiang: one real source of height and storeys, Wenzhou-Ruian's 小区单体化成果信息 (roof height, ground height and total floors per building; 491 records, 4 rows opened, no coordinates), and a Fuyang building register (25,715 records, no height). So even the platforms that do hold building items publish codes, registers and small samples, never a city footprint layer with heights; the 3D city models behind "实景三维中国" stay inside government networks. None of the entries read carries a licence field; the one platform terms text read in full (Shenzhen's) allows personal use only while the data stays online and bars redistribution.
- **Two other academic sets opened or read.** SinoBF-1 (figshare 29262584, CC BY 4.0, 109 cities, OPENED one city: footprints plus a numeric function class, no height) and CBRA (Zenodo 7861676, CC BY 4.0, a 2.5 m rooftop-area raster, page only). CUFB-China, the urban-village set and the GEDI record derive from CMAB or are summaries. GABLE was searched by name on Zenodo, figshare and the web: nothing found under that name.
- **GlobalBuildingAtlas covers China and Mongolia** (see the GLOBAL row): all land tiles exist, heights are modelled and CC BY-NC, and the Ulaanbaatar tile was opened.

**Mongolia**

The portal's whole catalogue was read: opendata.gov.mn's own backend call (GET /apiv1/dataset-file, found in its page scripts) returns all 738 dataset files in one answer (the older JSON-LD feed and its ignored `page` parameter showed only 100). Titles, organizations, file types, licences (735 CC0, 3 CC BY) and the column lists of the nearest items were read: no building footprint, height or storey layer, no 3D city model; the nearest are a parcel table without geometry, a five-row social-infrastructure building count and a three-row address fund. ALAGaC's page links its geoportal at geoportal.nsdi.gov.mn/alagac/home/, which does not connect from here (Wayback offline), so whatever geometry sits behind that geoportal is unverified. The two ACRS papers describe LOD3 research for Ulaanbaatar with no data link. The only per-building height found for Ulaanbaatar is GlobalBuildingAtlas (CC BY-NC, modelled; the Ulaanbaatar tile was opened and gives 18,898 buildings with a height in the Ulaanbaatar Overture box, median 3.4 m).

**Global sets**

Licences and coverage are from the pages named in B.4. The Middle East has no Google Open Buildings coverage and no 2.5D Temporal coverage (the project pages list South and South-East Asia, Africa and Latin America only). Microsoft covers most of Asia and the Middle East except China, Korea and Taiwan, with heights named for Turkey and a few others. GlobalBuildingAtlas is the only global per-building height set that covers China and Mongolia (its index files show every land tile present), but its heights and LoD1 are CC BY-NC.

### 7.3 South-East and Central Asia

**Singapore**

Opened: HDB Existing Building (13,509 polygons; fields OBJECTID, BLK_NO, ST_COD, ENTITYID, POSTAL_COD), HDB Property Information (13,392 rows), the URA Master Plan 2025 height-control layer (982 polygons), and the NUS CityJSON (10,966 CityObjects, attributes read). Pages only: MP2025 and MP2019 building layers, NParks, JTC, OneMap 3D. The data.gov.sg dataset search is not a query API (the `query` parameter was ignored), so I paged the whole dataset list (468 pages) and filtered names for building, footprint, storey, 3D, height, lidar, DSM, DTM: 59 hits, all read by name; none is a 3D model or LiDAR. The lab guide states the open 3D city models are not really available and OneMap 3D cannot be downloaded.

**Malaysia**

Pages and APIs only; nothing downloaded. MyGDI shares data by application. Sarawak's CKAN catalogue and map server were searched through their APIs (no building data; open terms read). Selangor (SelGDX), Penang, Sabah, Kuala Lumpur (DBKL) and the other states are in Nothing better and Unverified: most Malaysian government hosts timed out from this network (000 after 20 to 30 s), Cloudflare answered 403 for one news site, and the Sabah state sites answered a "URL blocked" page with HTTP 500. I did not get round them. JUPEM and NRES home pages were fetched and name no building download.

**Indonesia**

The BIG ArcGIS server (geoservices.big.go.id/rbi) lists 25 folders; 18 answer without a token (BANGUNANDANFASUM, BASEMAP, BATASWILAYAH, GARISPANTAI, HIDROGRAFI, HIPSOGRAFI, Hosted, IGT, INDEKS, PENUTUPLAHAN, PERAIRAN, RBI_SULSEL, RBI_SULTRA, REFERENSISPASIAL, TOPONIMI, TRANSPORTASI, TRANSPORTASIDANUTILITAS, VEGETASI) and 7 return "Token Required" (DATABASE, RBI_GORONTALO, RBI_SULBAR, RBI_SULTENG, RBI_SULUT, UJICOBA, Utilities), which I did not try to get past. In Hosted the only building-height products are the six LoD1 I3S layers (Makassar, Manado, Bitung, Kendari, Bantaeng, Sulsel); the other 59 services are vector-tile packages, a few feature layers, a point cloud and a road scene layer. Per-layer feature totals and NDSMHMAX statistics come from each layer's statistics endpoint. The LoD1 services are I3S scene layers whose attribute buffers (one per field) are plain binary; the geometry is Draco. I decoded the Draco geometry of the Makassar leaf nodes with DracoPy to place each feature (vertex offsets are metres from the node centre; in a random sample of 3,000 features the nearest Overture footprint to the centroid is 1.1 m away at the median and 85 % are within 10 m, so placement is sound). Two caveats: (1) 435 leaf nodes cover the box plus a margin and yield 309,564 features of which 165,776 have a distinct OBJECTID, so the same building recurs across nodes; I de-duplicated on OBJECTID and counted 106,440 in the box, more than Overture's 80,773 buildings there; (2) NDSMHMAX is a field name I interpret; no metadata states it, and Bantaeng's values are clipped to 1 to 10 m.

Jakarta is the largest find: the ArcGIS Server behind the Jakarta Satu geoportal (tataruang.jakarta.go.id/server) lists 118 folders; Master_Bangunan answers queries without a token with 1,741,931 building polygons, but floor counts (JMLLNT) are recorded on 0.65 % of them and no metre height; a roof-form model of 12 kelurahan has metre heights on 62,223 parts; Kota Tua has a 3D layer with metre heights (25,165 buildings; its storey and year fields are constants). The public 3D map's own layer answers "Token Required". Licences are not stated anywhere I could read. The Jakarta portal Satu Data Jakarta has a search API (POST, key "q") that I used for the keyword counts; other Indonesian regional data (Kaltim CKAN) held one footprint set (Balikpapan, 185,364 polygons, public domain, no heights). BIG's national portal terms and the regulations on data sharing were unreachable (see Unverified).

**Philippines, Thailand, Vietnam, Myanmar, Cambodia, Laos, Brunei, Timor-Leste**

The 2020 ISPRS survey (Biljecki) found no country with open building data over its territory and no open LiDAR in the region at that time; its OSM completeness table gives buildings with height / storeys: Indonesia 0.0 % / 0.4 %, Malaysia 0.4 % / 4.4 %, Philippines 0.0 % / 0.5 %, Singapore 1.0 % / 15.5 %, Thailand 0.2 % / 2.2 %, Vietnam 0.0 % / 1.1 %, Brunei 0.0 % / 8.2 %, Myanmar 0.0 % / 1.6 %. That is 2020, before the Overture sample here.

**Central Asia**

Reached and searched: data.egov.kz (site search), data.gov.kg (CKAN API), Brunei and the Open Development Mekong and Myanmar CKAN catalogues. Not reached from this network: the other national and city portals (data.gov.uz, data.gov.tj, almaty.gov.kz, astana.gov.kz, geoportal.kz, cadastre.uz and the rest). Tashkent's city site geoblocks Canada. The addresses of the national portals of Thailand, Uzbekistan, Kazakhstan, Brunei, Cambodia and Myanmar come from the Wikidata official-website field of their open-data-portal items (queried in this run); Kyrgyzstan's, the Philippines' and Vietnam's national portal addresses are those of the first pass, whose origin was not recorded.

### 7.4 South Asia and the Middle East

**India**

GOBS was described from its three pages and not opened: access is by request form with e-mailed link, so no sample was fetched. Its height is Google's 2.5D Temporal value and its floors are that height divided by 3, so it adds no independent measurement. NUIS was read through Wayback because www.nrsc.gov.in/readmore_NUIS answers 404 live; it says 152 towns at 1:10,000, thematic, with no height. GOBS's terms were read in full from the Get Data page (the table's licence cell): non-commercial, stated purpose, no redistribution of raw files, attribution to GOBS, Google Open Buildings and OSM, and the upstream licences bind the user. The national data portal (data.gov.in) answers nothing from here (000, and every other Indian government host that was probed was either reachable or refused the connection outright, which looks like a block on foreign addresses); its pages were read through Wayback, but the catalogue search cannot be queried, so datasets with "building" in them could not be listed (see UNVERIFIED). Its licence, GODL-India, was read from the 2017 Gazette notification. What was learned from the state and city portals: no Indian state or city portal reached lists a building outline layer. Kerala's KSDI (a GeoNode with 484 datasets) and Pune's portal (238 datasets, read in full) have none; Odisha's 4K GEO holds a 1:4000 cadastral database behind a login; Mumbai, Hyderabad, Chennai, Ahmedabad, Delhi and Bengaluru have no open building layer on any page that answered. Survey of India's Open Series Maps are 1:50,000 topographic sheets behind a captcha; SVAMITVA's portal returned an empty page. Telangana's open data portal is listed in an OSM wiki page that names only an amenities point set for GHMC.

**Abu Dhabi**

The Building layer is the one official, per-building, open-looking storey source found in the region. Its service description is generic text; the fields and counts are from the layer's JSON. Floors are filled on 50.1 % of 392,495 records, and the source column shows photogrammetry or field survey on the records sampled. The REST endpoint served 4,546 features for one box without a key. Licence: the service has no copyright text, and the Abu Dhabi Open Data Platform at data.abudhabi (a Drupal and React site whose licence page is a script-built route) publishes its licence in its Drupal JSON:API, which was read: the Abu Dhabi Government Open Data Licence v1.0, attribution only, commercial use included (see the table). The platform's catalogue search answered HTTP 500 and the full metastore listing timed out, so whether layer 353 is one of its datasets is not established; the licence is therefore stated as applying if the layer is covered, and the service itself says nothing.

**Dubai and the other emirates**

Dubai Municipality's twin has 3D models of more than 195,000 buildings but is reached through a portal for government entities, partners, private companies and students. Dubai Pulse's building statistics set is obsolete and aggregate; Dubai Municipality's own Pulse tables are a floor-level register (one row per building floor with usage, units and area; 332 MB) and a usages table, with no coordinates, behind a permission request. Fujairah's GIS Center describes a 3D project; nothing downloadable was seen. Ajman's open data portal (195 datasets, CC BY 4.0) has building-licence statistics only and Ajman's spatial layers are by official letter; RAK's GIS Center markets layer data, maps and aerial photos; no Sharjah or Umm Al Quwain portal page was found. Dubai Pulse itself does not connect from here, so the pages were read from Wayback captures.

**Israel**

Beer Sheva is the one open Israeli per-building floors layer found. The government portal's Mapi (Survey of Israel) organization has 117 datasets, none a building layer (parcels, blocks, sheet indexes, 2015 aerial sheets, trees, schools). Other building-titled data.gov.il sets are Beer Sheva's preserved buildings, green buildings (environment ministry) and a Ma'ale Adumim public-buildings table. Tel Aviv's GIS server and website answer with a maintenance page (HTTP 571) and its Wayback copy of the open data portal answered 472.

**Cyprus**

The curated GeoPackage was opened. Check the floor field before use: 61 % of values are "1", the 2014 imagery misses newer buildings, and floors agreed with Overture's num_floors on only 18.7 % of 1,433 pairs. Source and Overture counts differ strongly in the Nicosia box (16,321 against 26,896) (the reason was not examined); 65.6 % of source points fall in an Overture polygon.

**Bahrain, Oman**

Bahrain's open data portal (OpenDataSoft) lists 515 datasets; its building sets are 2020 census tables by governorate (ownership, type, usage), not geometry. SLRB sells 1:1000 topographic data at BD 40 per half km2 under a licence agreement; its topographic leaflet (read) says the buildings layer is footprints and rooftops at LOD1 to LOD1.5, ±20 cm, across 478 blocks that complete the Kingdom, currency 3 years: the best-specified building product in the Gulf, but sold, not open. Oman's data portal is a statistics portal of the NCSI, and the national survey authority's geoportal (Wayback copy) lists a product catalogue and free downloads whose contents were not captured. Qatar's open data portal (2,082 datasets, CC BY) and Riyadh Municipality's data sets hold statistics and asset lists, not footprints.

## 8. Nothing better found

### 8.1 East Asia (Japan, Koreas, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau)

**Japan, 47 prefectures**

Method: for each prefecture two BODIK queries ("<prefecture> 建物", "<prefecture> 都市計画基礎調査 建築物 3D"), kept only datasets published by that prefecture; G-spatial full-text queries per prefecture (see example URLs); the national catalogue data.e-gov.go.jp (answered 2xx; returns national ministries only). Result: nothing building-polygon-level more open or newer than PLATEAU in any prefecture except as listed in the TABLES.

| Prefectures | Result | Pages read |
|---|---|---|
| Hokkaido, Aomori, Iwate, Miyagi, Akita, Yamagata, Fukushima, Ibaraki, Tochigi, Gunma, Saitama, Chiba, Niigata, Toyama, Ishikawa, Fukui, Yamanashi, Nagano, Gifu, Aichi, Mie, Shiga, Hyogo, Nara, Tottori, Shimane, Okayama, Hiroshima, Yamaguchi, Tokushima, Kagawa, Ehime, Kochi, Saga, Nagasaki, Kumamoto, Miyazaki, Kagoshima, Okinawa | no building product by the prefecture found by these searches (G-spatial: point clouds for Yamanashi and Ishikawa-Noto only); PLATEAU units exist in 37 of these 39 (Yamagata and Fukui none) | https://data.bodik.jp/api/3/action/package_search?q=%E5%BB%BA%E7%89%A9%20GIS ; https://www.geospatial.jp/ckan/api/3/action/package_search?q=%E9%9D%99%E5%B2%A1%E7%9C%8C%20%E7%82%B9%E7%BE%A4&rows=0 |
| Tokyo | the 3D digital map (same as PLATEAU 2025) and land-use survey GIS, both in TABLES | https://catalog.data.metro.tokyo.lg.jp/api/3/action/package_search?q=3D |
| Kanagawa | point clouds (cc-by) only; the 都市計画基礎調査 (第11回) packages on BODIK are city-level (Atsugi, Yokosuka: ZIP/XLS, not opened) | https://www.geospatial.jp/ckan/api/3/action/package_show?id=kanagawa-2024-pointcloud ; https://data.bodik.jp/api/3/action/package_search?q=%E9%83%BD%E5%B8%82%E8%A8%88%E7%94%BB%E5%9F%BA%E7%A4%8E%E8%AA%BF%E6%9F%BB%20%E5%BB%BA%E7%89%A9 |
| Shizuoka | VIRTUAL SHIZUOKA point clouds (cc-by or ODbL by record); 35 PLATEAU units (mostly LOD1) | https://www.geospatial.jp/ckan/api/3/action/package_show?id=shizuoka-2025-pointcloud |
| Kyoto, Wakayama | Kyoto's BODIK entries are building-construction statistics (RDF/CSV/XLSX); Wakayama has a PLATEAU page for Wakayama city and point clouds of two temples on G-spatial (kozanji, toukeijinja); PLATEAU units: Kyoto 3, Wakayama 3 | https://data.bodik.jp/api/3/action/package_search?q=%E5%BB%BA%E7%89%A9%20GIS |
| Osaka, Fukuoka, Oita | Osaka and Fukuoka have only HTML survey pages on BODIK; Oita has the aggregate packages in TABLES | https://data.bodik.jp/api/3/action/package_search?q=%E9%83%BD%E5%B8%82%E8%A8%88%E7%94%BB%E5%9F%BA%E7%A4%8E%E8%AA%BF%E6%9F%BB%20%E5%BB%BA%E7%89%A9 |
| Big cities with their own 3D data | Sapporo, Sendai, Saitama, Chiba, Yokohama, Kawasaki, Sagamihara, Niigata, Shizuoka, Hamamatsu, Nagoya, Kyoto, Osaka, Sakai, Okayama, Hiroshima, Kitakyushu, Fukuoka and Kumamoto have PLATEAU units (Kobe has none; Hyogo's are Himeji, Kakogawa, Miki, Tatsuno, Asago) | the catalogue list in 4.1.1 |

Hyogo has no building product beyond its five PLATEAU units in what was searched.

**South Korea, 17 provinces and metropolitan cities**

Method: data.go.kr dataset search ("<unit> 건물" and "<unit> 건축물 3D"), kept titles of that unit with 건물, 건축, 3D or 공간정보. Example answered URL: https://www.data.go.kr/tcs/dss/selectDataSetList.do?keyword=%EC%84%9C%EC%9A%B8%ED%8A%B9%EB%B3%84%EC%8B%9C+%EA%B1%B4%EB%AC%BC&currentPage=1&perPage=20 . The portals of the units were also loaded as home pages (2xx): data.seoul.go.kr, data.gg.go.kr, data.busan.go.kr, data.daegu.go.kr, data.incheon.go.kr, data.ulsan.go.kr, and the province home pages (gwangju, daejeon, sejong, chungbuk, chungnam, jeonbuk, jeonnam, gb, jeju .go.kr); data.gwd.go.kr and www.gyeongnam.go.kr did not answer. The home pages show no building catalogue; the dataset searches are the evidence. The same searches were rerun through data.go.kr's file search (`selectDataSetList.do?keyword=...&dType=FILE`: 공간정보 건물, 새주소건물, 건축물 공간정보 shp, 건물 층수 SHP) and listed the city and county sets named in TABLES; the unit portals' own catalogues (Busan, Daegu, Daejeon, Incheon, Ulsan, Gwangju, Gyeonggi) were not searched one by one.

**Taiwan cities**

Taichung publishes its building shapefile (see TABLES) and its older versions (107, 111, 113); New Taipei, Kaohsiung, Tainan and Taoyuan: data.gov.tw's catalogue (queried with the site's own search body) lists no building footprint or floors-per-building dataset for them, only permit, inspection, parking and utility tables (examples in TABLES). The cities' own portals were not loaded again (see UNVERIFIED).

**Macau**

The whole data.gov.mo catalogue (1,377 datasets, read through the portal's own search API) holds no building geometry: 29 datasets from 土地工務局 (project lists, land area, coastline, hill heights; XLS or XLSX), one shapefile (bus routes), none flagged as spatial.

### 8.2 China, Mongolia and the global sets

**China: units with nothing better than CMAB, 3D-GloBFP, GlobalBuildingAtlas and Overture**

Addresses are the ones on the fetched 2021 list of local platforms (https://www.cnblogs.com/Deyunshe/p/14850638.html) and, for Chongqing, a search result naming data.cq.gov.cn/rop/home; no host was guessed. data.gov.cn, the national platform whose local-platform list would be the better source, does not connect from here and Wayback was offline. "Nothing better" for the units that did not answer means no building product was seen, not that none exists.

- **Catalogue read, nothing usable beyond the table rows:** Beijing (all 4,730 datasets paged; none a footprint or 3D layer), Guangdong (so Guangzhou, Shenzhen, Zhuhai, Foshan, Huizhou, Dongguan and the other cities on 开放广东: Shenzhen's code-name-address list and registers only), Zhejiang (so Hangzhou, Wenzhou, Shaoxing and the other cities on its platform: the Ruian per-building heights and floors, 491 records, and a Fuyang register).
- **Home page read, no catalogue behind it:** Yunnan (data.yn.gov.cn is the integrated public-data platform; the text of its open-data portal entry says 敬请期待, coming soon), Henan (data.hnzwfw.gov.cn/odweb redirects to the province's services home page, no open-data page), Suzhou (www.suzhou.gov.cn/dataOpenWeb/data redirects to the city home page).
- **Answered but blocked:** Shanghai (data.sh.gov.cn answers 412 Precondition Failed to curl and to WebFetch; the Wayback capture of 2025-06-09 is a 1,000-character shell), Tianjin (data.tj.gov.cn answers 403 with a 13-byte body; Wayback capture of 2026-03-02 titled 天津市数据局, 1,850 characters, no catalogue), Inner Mongolia (gzw.nmg.gov.cn/zwgk/zdlyxxgk/sjkf answers 403 with a 7,722-byte page, not read for data). A block is not worked round.
- **No connection:** Chongqing (data.cq.gov.cn times out), Fujian (data.fujian.gov.cn), Shandong (data.sd.gov.cn), Guizhou (data.guizhou.gov.cn, also named by Guizhou's own annual report table) and Ningxia (ningxiadata.gov.cn) resolve but time out on TCP, as do the city platforms Xiamen, Qingdao, Jinan, Ningbo, Wuhan (wuhandata.gov.cn), Chengdu (cddata.gov.cn), Guiyang, Harbin and Changsha, and the provinces Hainan, Jiangxi, Shaanxi, Sichuan (scdata.net.cn) and Xinjiang; data.gz.gov.cn (Guangzhou's own host) does not resolve and opendata.sz.gov.cn (Shenzhen's own host) resets the connection. Source-address filtering is the likely cause (some Chinese hosts answer, others do not); no way round was tried. The timeouts were checked by name resolution and a TCP attempt on six of them (Fujian, Shandong, Chongqing, Guizhou, Guangzhou, Shenzhen) and by plain fetch on the rest.
- **No address on any fetched page:** Hebei, Shanxi, Liaoning, Jilin, Heilongjiang (only Harbin city is listed), Jiangsu (only Suzhou and Changzhou), Anhui (only Hefei, Bengbu, Huangshan), Hubei (only Wuhan), Hunan (only Changsha), Guangxi, Gansu, Qinghai, Xizang, and the cities Nanjing, Hangzhou (reached through the Zhejiang platform) and Xi'an. The one Qinghai capture read earlier (a 2025 Wayback capture of data.qinghai.gov.cn) is a default web-server page.
- **Search engine results** (not evidence, listed as pointers): a Hubei provincial real-estate market-supervision data platform (launched 26 August, building and unit level, offered to banks through the province's authorized-operation platform; the news page read confirms this and says nothing on price): not an open dataset. A search answer for the national building-height shapefiles of one blog (CSDN) offers per-city vector heights for Nanjing, Wuhan and Shanghai among others: third-party redistribution of unknown origin and terms, not opened and not counted.

**Mongolia**

The national portal opendata.gov.mn was read whole (738 files): nothing on buildings beyond a parcel table without geometry. ALAGaC's geoportal host (geoportal.nsdi.gov.mn) does not connect from here. No Ulaanbaatar city open-data portal was found: a search answer turned up only a 2014 plan for a CKAN portal and an IFC advisory project on the city's data management, no live address; the city's 33 files (zones, forest and river-protection boundaries) are on the national portal. The two ACRS Ulaanbaatar papers have no data link.

### 8.3 South-East and Central Asia

**Indonesia, the 38 provinces**

The list of the 38 provinces and their official sites comes from Wikidata (an instance-of-province query run in this run; Central Papua and South Papua have no website recorded). Each homepage was fetched and its links searched for data, geoportal, satudata and sdi hosts. Reached with a data link: Kepulauan Bangka Belitung (sdi.babelprov.go.id, a statistical-indicator catalogue read through its daftar-data page, no building product), Kalimantan Tengah (satudata.kalteng.go.id, its dataset search page loads without results by script and shows a count of 0), Kalimantan Timur (data.kaltimprov.go.id, a CKAN portal: 198 datasets for "bangunan", of which one set has geometry, the Balikpapan building footprints in the Tables above; the rest are tables, PDF maps and the Samarinda register), Maluku (the homepage links satudataindonesia.malukuprov.go.id, which does not resolve, and an ArcGIS Hub page that is an empty shell of 2,245 bytes), Bali (balisatudata.baliprov.go.id, whose menu has Peta Geospasial and Peta Tata Ruang; its search is script-driven and returned no results page to read), DKI Jakarta (see the Tables). Banda Aceh (data.bandaacehkota.go.id, address from Wikidata) loads a script shell; Bandung's data.bandung.go.id (Wikidata) timed out. Homepages fetched with no data portal link: Aceh, Riau, Sumatera Utara (a PPID link only), Kepulauan Riau (PPID only), DI Yogyakarta (1.8 KB page), Kalimantan Utara, Nusa Tenggara Timur, Gorontalo, Papua, Papua Barat, Papua Pegunungan, Papua Barat Daya. Homepages that did not answer: Bengkulu, Jambi, Sumatera Selatan, Banten, Jawa Tengah, Jawa Barat, Nusa Tenggara Barat, Sulawesi Tengah, Sulawesi Utara, Sulawesi Tenggara (no connection); Lampung, Kalimantan Selatan, Kalimantan Barat, Maluku Utara (403); Sumatera Barat (503), Jawa Timur (525), Sulawesi Selatan (522), Sulawesi Barat (a 114-byte page). The first pass had read landing pages by guessed hostnames (Aceh satudata and geoportal, Riau geoportal.riau.go.id, Kepulauan Riau satudata, Sulawesi Utara satudata with a "bangunan" search page, Maluku Utara, Sulawesi Tengah, Sumatera Selatan, Jawa Tengah, DI Yogyakarta geoportal with its services directory disabled (403), Jawa Timur data.jatimprov.go.id, Banten an 88-byte page, and the "Geoportal Palapa" shell for seven provinces; Lampung, Kalimantan Selatan and Kalimantan Barat, Jawa Barat and the Sumatera Utara geoportal were blocked); those pages are kept as pages read, not as evidence of absence, and none showed a building product with heights or storeys. National catalogue data.go.id was searched for bangunan, gedung lantai, tinggi bangunan, footprint and shapefile bangunan: tables of counts and asset registers only. The only subnational building-height products found are BIG's (Sulawesi) and Jakarta's.
Pages: https://data.kaltimprov.go.id/dataset/?q=bangunan ; https://sdi.babelprov.go.id/katalog/daftar-data ; https://satudata.kalteng.go.id/dataset?q=bangunan ; https://balisatudata.baliprov.go.id/search-data?q=bangunan ; https://data.go.id/dataset?q=bangunan ; https://satudata.sulutprov.go.id/dataset?q=bangunan ; https://geoportal.riau.go.id ; https://satudata.acehprov.go.id ; https://geoportal.jogjaprov.go.id

**Malaysia states**

Johor: the state portal page of terms read, no building dataset. Sarawak: the CKAN catalogue and the iServer map server were searched, no building data (open terms read; see the Tables). Selangor: selangor.gov.my reachable; the SelGDX platform (launched October 2023) is named in news pages, whose own addresses were not found in the text read; geoportal and data hosts tried did not connect. Penang: portal hosts did not connect; the Internet Archive has only a 2019 copy of data.penang.gov.my (a generic open-data portal, no datasets read); the Penang open-data page of the land office (ptg.penang.gov.my) timed out. Sabah: sabah.gov.my and dbkk.sabah.gov.my answered a block page; the GeoFusion page loads (platform features only). Kuala Lumpur (DBKL): portal reachable, site map only; the KL 3D model in the news is geological. Putrajaya (open-data PDF list, no building data). No Malaysian state or city source with building data was found.

## 9. Non-governmental sources

### 9.1 East Asia (Japan, Koreas, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau)

- Overture Maps buildings (release 2026-10-21.0): in these 8 boxes footprints come from OpenStreetMap (ODbL), Microsoft ML Buildings (Tokyo, Osaka, rural Japan only) and `doi:10.5281/zenodo.8174931`; heights only from OSM (see OVERTURE).
- East Asian buildings dataset (CLSM, Zenodo 8174931): "A first high-quality vector data of buildings in East Asian countries based on a comprehensive large-scale mapping framework", published 2023-07-22, CC BY 4.0, one zip of 23,108,515,351 bytes; description names China, Japan, North Korea, South Korea, Mongolia; no height field is stated in the record (not opened). Overture uses it for footprints in Seoul, Busan, Taipei, Kaohsiung, Hong Kong boxes. Licence allows personal cache with attribution; ODbL-mixable: CC BY, generally compatible (not a legal finding) (https://zenodo.org/api/records/8174931).
- GlobalBuildingAtlas GBA.LoD1 (TUM): heights and LoD1 for the globe; the Hugging Face repository states "license: cc-by-nc-4.0" and says its polygons come from Google Open Buildings, CLSM (Zenodo 8174931) and its own derivation, so Japan, Korea and Taiwan are within a global set (NC licence: not mixable with ODbL data in a shared product) (https://huggingface.co/datasets/vitrobot/GBA.LoD1/raw/main/README.md). Not opened in this run.
- 3D-GloBFP (Zenodo records 15459025, 15487037, 15487006): "the first global three-dimensional building footprint dataset", CC BY 4.0, published 2025; global, one row only; not opened (https://zenodo.org/api/records?q=3D-GloBFP&size=3).
- Microsoft ML Buildings: footprint source in Tokyo (235), Osaka (262) and rural Japan (289) Overture boxes; no height field in Overture for it; global dataset covered by another agent.
- OpenStreetMap: heights on 1.9 % (Tokyo), 59 % (Osaka), 4.0 % (Seoul), 1.6 % (Busan), 9.6 % (Taipei), 0.9 % (Kaohsiung), 3.2 % (Hong Kong), 0 % (rural Akita) of the Overture buildings in the boxes; ODbL. Osaka's heights equal PLATEAU's (see OVERLAP), which points to a PLATEAU import.
- Commercial (Zenrin, Z-Map, Google, HERE, 2GIS-like, Naver, Kakao, Baidu): no terms read in this run.

### 9.3 South-East and Central Asia

- OpenStreetMap: the footprint supplier in nearly every box; height tags on 0.0 to 12.4 % of buildings in the boxes (Singapore 12.4 %, Yogyakarta 3.6 %, Almaty 3.5 %, KL 3.0 %, Naypyidaw 2.6 %); ODbL.
- NUS Urban Analytics Lab HDB 3D model (Singapore): see the Singapore table; the lab's guide is the evidence that the island-wide model is not downloadable.
- Thinking Machines TM Open Buildings (12 Philippine cities, ODbL, hand-drawn, a coarse height class on 728 of 12,275).
- HOTOSM country exports (HDX, ODbL): OSM extracts.
- Open Development Mekong building footprints (Phnom Penh, Cambodia; CC BY-SA).
- Google Open Buildings and Microsoft ML Buildings: supplied inside Overture; Google: CC BY 4.0 or ODbL; Microsoft: seen as an Overture supplier only, licence not read here.
- GlobalBuildingAtlas: predicted heights, global.
- Commercial: KAZGISA digital twin offer (Kazakhstan).

### 9.4 South Asia and the Middle East

- **OpenStreetMap**: in these boxes OSM carries the only heights in Overture (all `height` values have no `/properties/height` source entry, so they are OSM records): Dubai Downtown 1,173 buildings (9.9 %), Tel Aviv 315 (1.7 %), Nicosia 678 (2.5 %); everywhere else under 1 %. Storeys are commoner: Dhaka 16,197 (23.9 %), Tel Aviv 3,156 (16.9 %), Dubai 1,348 (11.4 %), Kathmandu 7,516 (7.4 %). ODbL.
- **Google Open Buildings** (footprints in India, Pakistan, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka, Nepal in Overture): CC BY 4.0 and ODbL 1.0, both stated on the pages read.
- **Microsoft ML Buildings**: the main footprint supplier in Tehran (56 %), Riyadh (85 %), Baghdad (51 %) and half of Amman; no heights from it in any box.
- **GOBS (AEEE)**: see table 1; non-commercial, by request, no redistribution of raw files, ODbL-bound upstream.
- **Dhaka rooftop solar potential (World Bank energydata.info)**: CC0 1.0 per its CKAN record (read); a 47 MB shapefile of rooftop footprint polygons with building type and rooftop solar attributes for a sample area of central Dhaka (the record says "sample area"), from 0.5 m stereo satellite imagery, plus a 134 MB attributes CSV; no height or storey field is named on the record; not opened. Personal offline cache yes; ODbL-mixable yes (CC0).
- **University of Cyprus curated GeoPackage**: CC BY 4.0.
- Not fetched in this run (leads from searches only, so unverified): OpenBuildingMap (GFZ), Open Cities Kathmandu footprints (126,105 per a World Bank report that answered 403), AW3D commercial 3D vectors, CGTrader and RenderHub city models.

## 10. The Overture sample

### 10.1 East Asia (Japan, Koreas, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau)

Method: release 2026-10-21.0 (S3 listing https://overturemaps-us-west-2.s3.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&delimiter=/&prefix=release/ ); duckdb with httpfs over `s3://overturemaps-us-west-2/release/2026-10-21.0/theme=buildings/type=building/*` and a filter on the `bbox` struct (row-group statistics prune the parts; files and row-group counts not logged: not the pyarrow route of Europe-E section 9). Height supplier = the record's `sources[1].dataset` (no per-property source entries in these boxes).

| Region and box | File | Row groups read | Buildings | With height | With num_floors | No height and no floors | Height supplied by (share of buildings with a height); footprints by |

| Osaka (Kita/Chuo), 135.48..135.53 x 34.67..34.72 | glob | not logged | 48,576 | 28,773 (59.2 %) | 4,044 (8.3 %) | 16,108 (33.2 %) | OpenStreetMap 28,773 (100 %). Footprints: OpenStreetMap 47,764; CLSM 550; Microsoft ML 262 |

Reading the table. Overture has almost no height in East Asia outside Osaka (0 to 10 %); in Osaka 59 % because OSM has PLATEAU's heights. Storeys are where OSM has them: 51 % in Hong Kong, 40 % in Taipei, 10 % in Tokyo, 7 % in Seoul, and none in rural Akita. Footprints are OSM almost everywhere, except Busan and Kaohsiung where CLSM supplies three quarters; no government dataset appears among Overture's suppliers in any box, so every one of PLATEAU, the Hong Kong Building layer, the Taipei overlay, Seoul's 2018 file, Gwacheon's polygons and Taichung's shapefile is new to it.

Reading the table. In all 14 boxes Overture's footprints come from three suppliers only: the East-Asia set (the Zenodo record 8174931, written in the sources as a doi), OpenStreetMap and, in Ulaanbaatar only, Microsoft ML. No Microsoft footprints appear in China (the Microsoft index has no China rows). Heights are only OSM tags: 0.0 to 1.6 % in all Chinese boxes but Nanjing (6.6 %, 934 buildings), none in rural boxes. Storeys are OSM tags too, 0.2 to 12.5 % in the boxes (Shenzhen 12.5 %, Guangzhou 9.1 %, Shanghai 7.8 %, Chongqing 7.3 %). Buildings with neither height nor floors are 87 to 100 % of every Chinese box and 92.3 % of Ulaanbaatar. In China that makes modelled heights (CMAB, 3D-GloBFP) the only per-building data, and a typical-height rule the fallback elsewhere.

Reading the table. (1) Overture's heights in this region are OSM records only: no box has a height from another supplier, and the share with a height is under 1 % in 35 of the 42 rows; the exceptions are Singapore centre (12.4 %), Yogyakarta (3.6 %), Almaty (3.5 %), Kuala Lumpur (3.0 %), Naypyidaw (2.6 %), Manila (1.3 %) and Tashkent (1.1 %). (2) Storeys exist mostly where OSM mappers added building:levels: Astana 39.1 %, Singapore centre 29.7 %, Johor Bahru 26.4 %, Almaty 21.7 %, but under 2 % in 21 of the 42 rows, among them Ho Chi Minh City (0.8 %), Da Nang, Hue, Vientiane, Kuching, Medan, Denpasar, Surabaya, the rural boxes, Makassar, Manado and Kendari. (3) Footprints come from OpenStreetMap, Google Open Buildings and Microsoft ML only; no national or city dataset supplies any footprint in these boxes. Google Open Buildings dominates in Ho Chi Minh City (70 %), rural Thailand (84 %), Hue (57 %) and Phnom Penh (58 %); OSM dominates in the Indonesian cities (Bandung 74 %, Denpasar 63 %, Makassar 83 %, Surabaya 85 %, Jakarta 90 %). (4) The one place a better source exists (Makassar, Manado, Kendari) Overture carries 10, 2 and 2 heights in 80,773, 50,742 and 30,502 buildings.

| Kathmandu, 85.29..85.34 x 27.69..27.74 | part-00464 | 7 | 101,863 | 66 (0.1 %) | 7,516 (7.4 %) | 94,294 (92.6 %) | other (OSM record) 66 (100 %). Footprints: OpenStreetMap 91,635; Google Open Buildings 9,269; Microsoft ML 959 |

Reading it. In every box the only height Overture holds is an OSM record, and in 12 of 14 boxes it is under 2 % of buildings; only Dubai Downtown (9.9 %) and Nicosia (2.5 %) are higher. Footprint suppliers differ by region: Google Open Buildings dominates South Asia (Delhi 72 %, Karachi 77 %, Colombo 65 %, rural Bihar 79 %), Microsoft ML dominates Tehran, Riyadh and Baghdad, OSM dominates Dhaka (92 %), Kathmandu (90 %), Tel Aviv, Dubai and Nicosia. Storeys are the better-filled field: Dhaka 23.9 %, Tel Aviv 16.9 %, Dubai 11.4 %, Nicosia 8.2 %, but under 2 % in Delhi, Karachi, Tehran, Riyadh and rural Bihar, so an official storey or height layer would change the most in Delhi, Karachi, Riyadh and the rural box.

## 11. Overlap with Overture (what each source would add)

### 11.1 East Asia (Japan, Koreas, Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau)

Method: a source building is matched to the Overture building with the largest footprint overlap when the overlap exceeds 30 % of the smaller footprint; height = source's own height field (HK: TopHeight minus the lowest BaseHeight among the touching parts, the Overture building matched to the largest-overlap Tower polygon; PLATEAU: `measuredHeight` greater than 0; storeys: `storeysAboveGround` below 999, `bud_floor` above 0). "Adds" counts source buildings with a value where the matched Overture building has none, or nothing matches. Bytes: source figures are the file's uncompressed size (PLATEAU tiles were pulled by HTTP range from the multi-GB zips); Overture bytes not measured (duckdb).

| Osaka PLATEAU 2025 Umeda | 856 | 397 (46.4 %) | 321 = 69.9 % of 459 height-less | see above |

Reading. Where Overture is height-less (Tokyo, Hong Kong, Seoul), PLATEAU and the Hong Kong layer would give a height to 90-98 % of buildings inside their coverage; only the 306 PLATEAU units and Hong Kong are open for that. What the open Korean and Taiwanese files add is storeys, not heights: Seoul's 2018 file (storeys times 3.5 m as a height) adds storeys to 13,585 of 14,639 matched Overture buildings, Taichung's shapefile to 14,705, Gwacheon's to 2,017; the national Korean polygons with register attributes need V-World, which did not answer. Heights of Tokyo 2020 and OSM agree only to within 20 % in 59 % of the 49 pairs. Hong Kong's layer holds parts: matched to tower polygons its heights agree with OSM's whole-tower heights (median 2.6 m), while podium polygons set against whole buildings differ by tens of metres.

### 11.2 China, Mongolia and the global sets

Method: one representative point per source building (a point inside the footprint) in the box; the smallest Overture footprint containing it is the match; "adds a height" = the source has a height and the matched Overture building has none, or nothing matches. Height pairs are source against Overture's OSM-tagged heights (all Overture heights in these boxes are OSM). "Floors pairs" compare the source height with 3 m times Overture's num_floors. Box as in the Overture table. Bytes: compressed column chunks (id, bbox, height, num_floors, sources, geometry) of the row groups read.

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height (by supplier) | Source buildings that add a height Overture lacks | Height comparison where both | Bytes read |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3D-GloBFP tile 2435 (Shanghai and Zhejiang, EPSG:4326), Shanghai box 121.45..121.50 x 31.20..31.25 (Overture box: 18,694 buildings, 64 with a height) | 22,297 (all with a height; median 20.4 m; 10th/90th percentile 10.7/62.7 m); no storeys | 14,259 (63.9 %); 8,038 unmatched | 118 = 0.8 % of matched: other (OSM record) 118 | 22,179 = 99.5 % of source; where Overture has neither height nor floors: 20,529 = 92.1 % | 118 pairs: median abs diff 22.3 m, median signed (source minus Overture) -18.6 m, median ratio 0.79, within 20 % 31.4 %; against 3 m x floors (1,688 pairs): signed +6.6 m, ratio 1.30, within 20 % 29.6 % | Overture 8.3 MB (2 row groups) + source 68.2 MB zip (130 MB shp) |
| CMAB Shanghai (21 city shapefiles, EPSG:3857), same box | 9,126 (all with a height; median 26.4 m; 10th/90th 14.8/44.1 m); no storeys | 6,532 (71.6 %); 2,594 unmatched | 41 = 0.6 % of matched: other (OSM record) 41 | 9,085 = 99.6 % of source; where Overture has neither height nor floors: 8,242 = 90.3 % | 41 pairs: median abs diff 53.6 m, median signed -49.3 m, median ratio 0.47, within 20 % 9.8 % (the OSM-tagged ones are towers); against 3 m x floors (865 pairs): signed +4.5 m, ratio 1.22, within 20 % 19.5 % | Overture 8.3 MB + source 337 MB zip |
| CMAB Beijing (15 shapefiles), Beijing box 116.38..116.43 x 39.89..39.94 (Overture box: 31,833 buildings, 517 with a height) | 10,060 (median 17.6 m; 10th/90th 9.4/29.9 m); no storeys | 6,044 (60.1 %); 4,016 unmatched | 53 = 0.9 % of matched: other (OSM record) 53 | 10,007 = 99.5 % of source; where Overture has neither: 9,444 = 93.9 % | 53 pairs: median abs diff 7.0 m, median signed +5.8 m, median ratio 1.77, within 20 % 18.9 %; against 3 m x floors (564 pairs): signed +13.6 m, ratio 5.17, within 20 % 7.4 % (the old city's low-rise hutongs carry model heights near 17 m) | Overture 10.6 MB + source 249 MB zip |
| CMAB against 3D-GloBFP, Shanghai box (two modelled sets on the same footprints) | CMAB 9,202 polygons in the box by intersection | 6,880 CMAB centre points fall in a 3D-GloBFP footprint | n/a | n/a | 6,880 pairs: median abs diff 10.8 m, median signed (CMAB minus GloBFP) +4.5 m, median ratio 1.25, within 20 % 21.4 %, correlation 0.38 | the two files above |
| GlobalBuildingAtlas LoD1 tile e105_n50_e110_n45 (Source Cooperative Parquet copy, EPSG:4326 bbox column), Ulaanbaatar box 106.89..106.94 x 47.89..47.94 (Overture box: 19,623 buildings, 28 with a height, 1,503 with storeys) | 18,898 (all with a height; median 3.4 m; 10th/90th percentile 1.5/14.8 m); footprints from osm 14,725 and 3dglobfp 4,173; no storeys | not run (footprint matching to Overture was not done; counts only) | not run | not run: every GBA building has a height and Overture has one on 28 buildings | not run | 52 MB parquet (read in full, only source, height and bbox columns used) |

Reading. Neither modelled set has storeys, and both add a height to essentially every building in the box because Overture has none there. But in the same box they differ by a median 10.8 m and track each other poorly (correlation 0.38), so they are unfit to treat as per-building truth; they are best used as a typical-height fallback, and the CMAB sample count (9,126 against Overture's 18,694) shows its polygons merge blocks. Where Overture does have a height (towers tagged in OSM) the modelled values run 19 to 49 m too low in Shanghai and about 6 m too high in Beijing.

### 11.3 South-East and Central Asia

Method as buildings-europe-e.md section 10: a source building is matched to the Overture footprint that contains its centroid (for the CityJSON, the mean of its vertices; for the I3S layer, the mean of the decoded vertices of the feature); "adds" = the source has a value where the matched Overture building has none; pairs are heights where both exist. Release 2026-10-21.0.

**Source against Overture**

| Source and box | Source buildings | Matched to an Overture footprint | Matched with an Overture height (by supplier) | Source buildings that add a height Overture lacks | Height comparison where both | Bytes read |
